# Analytical Reservoir — Defendant Risk Ordered Master

**File:** `ANALYTICAL_RESERVOIR_DEFENDANT_RISK_ORDERED.md`  
**Ranking date:** 4 September 2026  
**Profiles retained:** 38  
**Ordering principle:** severity of evidential exposure for the defendant

## Controlling case statement

The patient entered Neurology in 2017 with an integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–function problem. Neurology identified severe young-onset Parkinsonism, a dystonic-looking lower-limb abnormality and genuine structural foot disease, but left the relationship between the neurological lower-limb disorder and the painful feet **“Not clear.”** Before discharge, the patient directly challenged the treating neurologist that the admission was incomplete because Neurology had not adequately returned to the painful feet. That Protest converted residual uncertainty into **known contested uncertainty**. The final synthesis omitted the Protest, preserved and strengthened patient-derived material supportive of separation — including that he had **“in his own mind — markedly divorced”** the foot pain from the leg tension — and transmitted an intelligent, counselled, understanding patient leaving with a **“clear plan.”** The document therefore preserved the chosen branch while removing the fact that the branch had been expressly contested before closure. Later evidence repeatedly crossed the same structural–neurological boundary without an identifiable global reconstruction. In 2025 the historical challenge returned, and the treating neurologist relied upon the continuity of the historical letters as evidence of his reasoning and shared understanding. **The hospital cannot rely on continuity as independent proof of the completeness of the material decision pathway because the continuity begins after the material contest has already been removed from the founding portable account.**

> **The Protest broke the shared understanding. The synthesis removed the break. The later record documented continuity across the gap.**

## Editorial and evidential rule

This is a claimant-side litigation master. It does not average the 38 profiles on an even keel. It orders them according to how directly they threaten the defendant's reliance on the longitudinal hospital record as a complete, fair and self-sufficient history of the material decision pathway.

For this master, the Protest is an established case fact. It occurred before documentary closure, was received by the treating neurologist, directly challenged the adequacy of the foot disposition, and was omitted from the final synthesis. Source status may still be recorded where relevant, but it is not used to weaken the substantive case by repeatedly restating the Protest conditionally.

Evidential discipline means limiting the claimant's proposition to what the record establishes. It does **not** mean affirmatively constructing the defendant's benign explanation. Failure to prove deliberate concealment is not proof of innocence; failure to prove one exclusive medical mechanism is not proof that the founding partition was reasonable; and later continuity is not independent corroboration where the later meaning is inherited from the founding representation.

Original profile numbers are retained for cross-reference. The substantive profile text is preserved except where earlier drafting explicitly decentered or conditionalised the Protest in a manner superseded by the controlling project understanding.


## Permanent causal hierarchy

This master is to be read as one case, not thirty-eight equal essays. The controlling sequence is:

> **integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–function presentation → selective strengthening of patient-derived separation → Protest → known contested uncertainty → omission of the decision fork → portable management account plus compatible patient knowledge-state → repeated later boundary-crossing evidence → local updating without global reconstruction → 2025 challenge → historical documentary continuity invoked to justify itself.**

The first nine risk-ranked profiles concern the reliability of the documentary pathway itself. The middle ranks show persistence, repeated testing, fragmentation and non-reconstruction. The lower ranks provide harm, context, proof discipline and synthesis. Motive remains last because the core case does not require proof of private intent.

The governing distinction is between **what later facts independently show** and **what later documents merely inherit or repeat from the founding representation**. Later clinical observations can be independent evidence. Later continuity of interpretation is not independent corroboration where its meaning descends from the same July 2017 account whose completeness is under challenge.

## Severity scale

- **CRITICAL — LOAD-BEARING:** capable of materially undermining the defendant's reliance on the longitudinal account itself.
- **VERY SERIOUS:** strong process or corroborative evidence showing persistence, cumulative notice, non-reconstruction or information loss.
- **SERIOUS:** important downstream mechanism or independent contradiction, but not alone dispositive of documentary reliability.
- **MATERIALITY / HARM / FAIRNESS:** strengthens consequence, predictability, harm or the minimum adequate alternative.
- **SYNTHESIS / EVIDENTIAL CONTROL / SECONDARY:** useful for proof discipline or presentation but adds less independent exposure.
- **FOUNDATIONAL CONTEXT:** necessary background rather than a major independent vulnerability.
- **LOWEST / INTENT-SENSITIVE:** unnecessary to the core case and most vulnerable to challenge if overstated.

# Risk-Ordered Profiles

# Risk Rank 01 — Original Profile 07

**Severity:** CRITICAL — LOAD-BEARING  
**Primary defendant exposure:** The Protest converts residual uncertainty into known contested uncertainty. Its omission removes the decision fork from the portable history.

## Profile 07 — Profile of the Protest

<!-- SOURCE: Pasted markdown(20260901-135422).md -->

<!-- Original source heading: Profile of the Protest -->

### The Event That Broke the Coherence Before the Record Restored It

The Protest occurred at a very particular point in the history.

It came **after** Neurology had already formed most of its clinical disposition, but **before** the durable account of that disposition had been completed and transmitted.

That position gives it its importance.

The Protest did not create the feet.

It did not create the dystonic-looking lower limb.

It did not create the uncertainty.

It did not create the Sinemet policy.

It did not create the separation of structural feet from the neurological management pathway.

Those things already existed.

What the Protest did was make the insufficiency of that arrangement **personally and expressly known to the treating neurologist before closure**.

That is the information event.

### Before the Protest

The GP had sent one integrated phenotype into hospital:

> **gait + feet + pathological tension + toe-clawing + failing movement and function.**

Neurology had then discovered substantially more.

It had severe genuine young-onset Parkinsonism.

It had an extraordinarily abnormal gait.

It had a pseudo-footdrop explicitly considered suggestive of dystonic posture.

It had structural foot disease.

It had longstanding painful feet.

It had PRKN/PARK2 under active consideration.

It had a department and specialist network possessing highly relevant movement-disorders expertise.

And it had a defensible decision to use considerable caution with levodopa and other dopaminergic treatment.

But it had not returned after the admission examination to resolve the structural-dynamic foot relationship.

The surviving synthesis placed the painful-foot problem in the space:

> **“may have been completely separate”**

or perhaps:

> **“a secondary peripheral neuropathy”**

followed by:

> **“Not clear.”**

Podiatry supplied the practical destination.

Before the Protest, therefore, the emerging management state could still be represented internally as:

> **a difficult case has been extensively investigated; Parkinsonism has been recognised; some residual uncertainty remains around the feet; there is nevertheless a sensible forward plan.**

The Protest destroys the innocence of that description.

### What the patient actually protested

The patient did not protest the diagnosis of Parkinsonism.

He did not protest because Sinemet had not been prescribed.

He did not tell Monaghan that he had PRKN.

He did not diagnose dystonia for the clinicians.

He did something much simpler.

As discharge approached, he deliberately caused Monaghan to be summoned and challenged the fact that after an extraordinarily extensive neurological admission the presenting feet had still not been dealt with.

The NCT/bald-tyres analogy expressed precisely that:

> **You have carried out the sophisticated examination, but you are about to send me away with the obvious unresolved problem still there.**

The project's existing formulation correctly identifies this as a challenge to **the sufficiency of the disposition of the feet**, not a rejection of the Parkinsonian diagnosis.

That distinction is essential.

The Protest attacked **completeness**.

### Why the Protest was so damaging to the emerging account

Until that point, the absence of a return to the feet was something the record could simply fail to speak about.

After the Protest, it could no longer remain merely an unnoticed absence in the mind of the treating neurologist.

The patient had made it explicit.

So the information state changed from:

> **the feet remain unresolved**

to:

> **the feet remain unresolved, and the patient has directly challenged the treating neurologist that this unresolved state represents an incomplete admission.**

Those are not equivalent states.

The first permits passive uncertainty.

The second creates **known contested uncertainty**.

That is the defining effect of the Protest.

### It also created a real fork

Before the Protest, the management pathway could simply continue.

After the Protest, there were visibly two possibilities.

#### Path one: reopen

Neurology could have:

-  returned to the feet;
-  reconsidered the original GP referral;
-  placed the dystonic-looking pseudo-footdrop beside the painful structural feet and toe phenomena;
-  sought specific movement-disorders input;
-  asked whether the structural and dynamic phenomena interacted;
-  preserved that relationship explicitly as an open neurological problem;
-  delayed physical discharge if necessary;
-  or explained and documented why further neurological examination was not warranted.

#### Path two: continue

The existing disposition could remain:

> **Parkinsonism managed neurologically;**

> **Sinemet/dopaminergic treatment cautiously deferred;**

> **feet routed structurally;**

> **relationship “Not clear.”**

Physical discharge followed the second path.

The importance of the Protest is that **from that moment onward the second path was no longer the only path generated by the clinical encounter**.

The patient had created another one.

The surviving discharge synthesis preserves the path that was taken.

It does not preserve that another path had been expressly demanded before closure.

That is why saying merely that “the Protest is missing from the notes” is much too weak.

> **What is missing is the fork in the road.**

### The Protest did not stop physical discharge

This is itself informative.

The patient physically left on 11 July.

There is no later examination of the feet during the eight-day admission.

There is no documented movement-disorders re-evaluation of the structural-dynamic relationship.

There is no record of the question being returned to the relevant senior expertise before the patient left.

The Protest therefore did not cause the clinical process to reopen.

That tells us something factual about its immediate effect:

> **it changed what was known, but it did not change the bedside disposition.**

### But physical discharge was not documentary closure

This is the second defining feature.

The durable account was still being produced.

The artefact itself proves that it remained open after the patient left. It contains:

> **“ADDENDUM at Transcription finalisation 2017-07-13th_21:41hrs”**

incorporates the 13 July DaT scan, apologises for the delay in getting the document out, and carries final consultant confirmation on 17 July.

Primary care independently confirms the transmission gap.

On 14 July Brennan records that he is:

> **“awaiting formal correspondence.”**

On 17 July, after the patient had already left hospital, Brennan still records:

> **“no formal discharge received as yet.”**

The extensive Monaghan correspondence appears in the GP record on 18 July.

So the sequence is:

> **Protest → physical discharge → document remains open → later information incorporated → GP waits → final consultant confirmation → authoritative synthesis travels downstream.**

We should not claim from that sequence alone that **the Protest caused the documentary delay**.

The document itself supplies another explanation for delay.

But the stronger proposition needs no such causal claim:

> **The Protest occurred before the final authoritative representation existed, and the author had time and documentary opportunity after acquiring that knowledge to determine what the finished account would carry forward.**

### This changes the significance of the later language

Once the Protest is restored to its correct chronological position, several sentences in the finished note cease to be ordinary descriptive remarks.

They become retrospective propositions about an admission whose coherence had already been challenged.

The document says it is intended:

> **“to consolidate a lot of discussions which we have had”**

to help:

> **“with debriefing”**

and:

> **“to set out for him a clear plan.”**

But the most consequential discussion about the adequacy of the foot disposition is not consolidated.

That is the first tell.

### “This has all suited us and Liam very well”

The note later describes the longer admission as having:

> **“suited us and Liam very well”**

because it enabled the team to explore many questions.

It says the prolonged admission produced:

> **“a firmer footing going forward for having a plan.”**

Before the Protest, this could simply be a positive retrospective description.

After the Protest, it has another evidential dimension.

The patient had personally stated that the admission had **not** resolved the thing he regarded as outstanding.

Yet the final account presents the duration of the admission as evidence of productive completeness and firmer footing.

The Protest is therefore the contrary information required to understand what that sentence does.

With the Protest:

> **many questions were explored, but the patient directly identified one central presenting question as unfinished.**

Without the Protest:

> **the unusually long admission appears to have successfully produced the basis for the plan.**

That difference is documentary, not rhetorical.

### The most remarkable tell: the patient's intelligence survives, but his question does not

The finished document repeatedly characterises the patient as:

> highly intelligent;

> exceptionally insightful;

> capable of retaining complex information;

> and someone who would:

> **“question very appropriately with exceptional insight.”**

The Protest fits that description perfectly.

An intelligent patient who had lived with the feet for years notices that after eight days nobody has returned to the problem and articulates the incompleteness through a simple analogy.

Yet the final document preserves:

> **the patient is an exceptionally appropriate questioner**

while omitting:

> **the exceptionally appropriate question he actually asked at the moment of discharge.**

That is an extraordinary selective effect.

The patient's cognitive credibility is retained.

The exercise of that credibility against the management plan is not.

### His “understanding” also changes meaning after the Protest

The final account says:

> **“Liam has a good understanding of why we wished to delay the commencement of levodopa or dopamine agonist type treatments…”**

That may be completely true.

But the Protest proves why **understanding cannot be treated as acceptance of the overall disposition**.

The patient could simultaneously understand:

> **why Neurology wanted to delay Sinemet**

and protest:

> **why are my feet still unresolved?**

Those propositions are perfectly compatible.

Yet because the Protest is absent, the patient-understanding language can give the finished account a broader appearance of consensual coherence than the bedside history warrants.

That is another effect of the omission.

### The Protest exposes where the real weakness was

It is especially significant that the patient did **not** attack the Sinemet policy.

That is the policy the final clinic note discusses with great humility.

The document admits:

> levodopa may work extremely well;

> perhaps sustainably;

> and perhaps:

> **“we may regret that we did not start this earlier.”**

That uncertainty is owned, explicit and defensible.

The Protest attacks something else:

> **the failure to finish the foot/lower-limb question.**

And that issue receives the weaker architecture:

> **“markedly divorced” → perhaps separate → perhaps neuropathy → “Not clear” → Podiatry.**

So the Protest identifies in real time the very asymmetry we have now found retrospectively.

> **The document is most explicitly self-critical about the defensible treatment decision.**
>
> The patient is most explicitly critical about the unresolved phenotype.**

The final synthesis carries the first criticism.

It does not carry the second.

### The Protest changes “Not clear”

This may be its single most important effect.

Without the Protest:

> **“Not clear”**

can mean:

> *after appropriate consideration we cannot determine the relationship.*

After the Protest, Monaghan knows another proposition:

> **the patient says the feet have not been sufficiently returned to for that uncertainty to count as a completed answer.**

Therefore the final document's continued use of “Not clear” has to be read alongside a suppressed distinction:

> **uncertainty after investigation**

versus:

> **uncertainty after non-return to the presenting feet, expressly challenged by the patient.**

The Protest does not prove which causal explanation was medically correct.

It changes the evidential meaning of the uncertainty.

### And the document remained capable of absorbing contrary information

This is what makes the production interval so significant.

The final synthesis was capable of accepting later objective information.

The DaT scan arrived after physical discharge.

It was incorporated.

Interpretation was added.

The document remained in production and was eventually confirmed several days later.

So the final artefact cannot be defended as a frozen bedside snapshot that simply happened to miss the Protest.

It was a **retrospective consolidation**.

The safe factual proposition is:

> **Later information capable of supporting the chosen Parkinsonian account was incorporated. The prior patient challenge capable of qualifying the completeness of the management account was not.**

Why that happened is a provenance and intent question.

The differential treatment of information is already observable.

### The Protest therefore changed the author-specific knowledge state

This is perhaps the cleanest forensic formulation.

Before the Protest, Monaghan could be said to know:

> **the feet remain unresolved.**

After the Protest, Monaghan knew:

> **the feet remain unresolved;**

> **the patient regards that as a failure to complete the presenting problem;**

> **the patient has expressly challenged the proposed disposition;**

> **and proceeding now means choosing not to reopen that issue before discharge.**

That is a materially different knowledge state.

And because Monaghan was also the author of the later-finalised synthesis, the distinction is actor-specific.

The same person:

> **heard the objection**

and subsequently:

> **authored the authoritative forward account.**

The documentary question is therefore not merely institutional:

> **What did the hospital know?**

It is:

> **What did the author know when he selected the information that became the final plan?**

### What the Protest did not do

For precision, the Protest did not:

-  establish PRKN;
-  establish that every foot lesion was neurological;
-  prove that Podiatry was inappropriate;
-  prove that Sinemet should have been started;
-  prove negligent treatment;
-  prove dishonest drafting;
-  prove why the document took until 17 July to reach final form.

Those propositions require different evidence.

Its significance is narrower and stronger.

The Protest established **notice and contest before finalisation**.

### What the Protest did

It did five things.

**First**, it converted silent incompleteness into explicit knowledge.

**Second**, it converted passive residual uncertainty into **contested uncertainty**.

**Third**, it created an alternative clinical path before closure.

**Fourth**, because the final account omitted it, it allowed the selected path to travel forward without the fact that its sufficiency had been challenged.

**Fifth**, because the final account was later-finalised, patient-facing and plan-setting, its omission affected not only the history but the **future information state inherited by the GP, patient and subsequent clinicians**.

That is its complete effect.

### The deepest effect: it should have prevented the document from proving itself

This is where the 2025 rebuttal makes the Protest even more important.

Eight years later Monaghan points the reviewer back to the 2017 correspondence and effectively says:

> **the reviewer will be able to see the clinical reasoning there.**

But the Protest tells us something indispensable about how those documents must be read.

They cannot simply prove:

> **the admission produced an understood, coherent, uncontested plan**

because before the final artefact was completed the patient had directly challenged the adequacy of one of its central dispositions.

Thus the Protest does not merely challenge the contents of the discharge synthesis.

It challenges **the discharge synthesis's later use as self-authenticating evidence of the completeness and reasonableness of the process that produced it.**

The finished document can certainly prove:

> **what Monaghan ultimately chose to record, explain and transmit.**

It cannot, by itself, prove:

> **that this was the complete and uncontested meaning of the bedside encounter.**

The source analysis now makes precisely that distinction.

### Profile of the Protest

> **The July 2017 Protest was not the origin of the clinical defect but the event that changed its evidential status. By the final day Neurology had already recognised severe young-onset Parkinsonism, a dystonic-looking pseudo-footdrop, structural painful feet and possible PRKN, while leaving the structural-dynamic foot relationship “Not clear” and routing the feet toward Podiatry under an otherwise coherent neurological management strategy. The patient did not challenge Parkinsonism or demand Sinemet. He deliberately summoned the treating neurologist and challenged the obvious incompleteness: after eight days of extensive investigation, the feet through which he had presented remained unresolved. From that moment the residual uncertainty was no longer merely residual; it was expressly contested to the clinician who would later author the definitive account. The Protest created a genuine fork — reopen the foot/lower-limb relationship or continue the existing disposition. Physical discharge proceeded without reopening it. But documentary closure had not occurred: the final patient-facing synthesis remained open after discharge, incorporated the later DaT result, was awaited by the GP on 14 and 17 July, and was finally confirmed on 17 July. In that later-finalised account the chosen path is represented as the product of an admission that “suited us and Liam very well,” had explored many questions, supplied “firmer footing,” produced a “clear plan,” and was understood by an exceptionally intelligent patient who questioned “very appropriately with exceptional insight.” What is absent is the exceptionally appropriate question he had actually asked. The Protest therefore does not merely identify a missing conversation. It identifies the missing fork that allowed a contested management disposition to be transmitted as an apparently coherent and uncontested one.**

And I think the irreducible version is:

> **Before the Protest, the feet were “Not clear.”**
>
> After the Protest, Monaghan knew that “Not clear” was not an adequate answer to the patient.**
>
> The final document nevertheless transmitted “Not clear,” the plan, and the patient's understanding — but not that knowledge.**

Or even more sharply:

> **The Protest did not change the diagnosis. It changed what the neurologist knew about the adequacy of his own disposition. The final synthesis changed the information state back.**

# Risk Rank 02 — Original Profile 20

**Severity:** CRITICAL — LOAD-BEARING  
**Primary defendant exposure:** Shows selective preservation of material that supports the chosen branch while the material contest is excluded from the durable account.

## Profile 20 — Documentary Selection

<!-- SOURCE: Pasted markdown (2)(3).md; source text preserved in full below. -->

<!-- Original series label: Profile 9 of 12 — Documentary Selection -->

### What Was Made Portable, and What Was Left Behind

The discharge synthesis did not simply record information.

It **selected** information for future use.

That distinction matters because a discharge document is not passive. It decides what the next clinician, the GP, the patient and later reviewers will be able to know without reconstructing the original encounter for themselves.

In this case, the selection was consequential.

A great deal was made highly portable.

One material part of the encounter was not.

### 1. What the document chose to carry forward

The finished synthesis carries an extraordinary amount of information.

It carries the severity of the Parkinsonism.

It carries the unusual youth of onset.

It carries the dystonic-looking pseudo-footdrop.

It carries the structural abnormalities of the feet.

It carries the long history.

It carries the possibility of PRKN.

It carries alternative genetic reasoning.

It carries the rationale for cautious dopaminergic treatment.

It carries the risks of dyskinesia and other complications.

It carries Podiatry.

It carries allied-health planning.

It carries the patient's intelligence.

It carries his questions.

It carries his ability to understand.

It carries the author's uncertainty.

It carries the author's confidence in the resulting management arrangement.

And it eventually carries:

> **“Not clear.”**

> **“firmer footing.”**

> **“clear plan.”**

> **“As per above.”**

So this is not a sparse document accidentally missing everything.

It is a document that **actively chooses what deserves durable representation**.

### 2. The patient's intelligence was selected for portability

The patient is repeatedly characterised as unusually intelligent, insightful and capable of asking appropriate questions.

That information becomes part of the permanent clinical story.

Why?

Because it helps the later reader understand that this was a patient capable of following complex neurological reasoning.

It also supports the proposition that the elaborate management plan was communicated to someone capable of understanding it.

Thus:

> **patient intelligence**

becomes portable.

> **patient insight**

becomes portable.

> **patient questioning**

becomes portable.

That is not inherently problematic.

The difficulty appears when we ask:

> **Which question was not made portable?**

### 3. The actual discharge-day question was left behind

The patient used exactly the capacities the document praises.

He recognised a mismatch.

After eight days of sophisticated investigation, the feet through which he had presented had not been returned to.

He caused Monaghan to be summoned.

He challenged the adequacy of the disposition.

That event demonstrates:

- observation;
- comprehension;
- appropriate questioning;
- ability to distinguish the neurological success of the admission from its unresolved component.

It is almost a perfect example of the patient's documented intellectual characteristics.

Yet the final document preserves:

> **he questions appropriately**

without preserving:

> **the question he asked when the plan was about to become final.**

That is documentary selection in its purest form.

### 4. Understanding was selected; disagreement was not

The note also carries the proposition that the patient understood why levodopa and dopamine agonists were being delayed.

Again, that can be true.

But understanding a treatment rationale does not mean accepting the completeness of the overall management disposition.

The patient could simultaneously believe:

> **I understand why you are delaying Sinemet**

and:

> **you still have not dealt with my feet.**

Those propositions do not conflict.

The final document makes the first portable.

It leaves the second behind.

The effect is that a later reader encounters a highly capable patient who understands the management reasoning but does not encounter the fact that the same patient contested the sufficiency of the foot management.

That alters the apparent doctor-patient relationship.

### 5. Clinician uncertainty was made portable

The document is unusually candid about uncertainty.

It preserves:

> possible PRKN;

> uncertain genetics;

> possible future regret about levodopa timing;

> uncertainty about the painful feet;

> “Not clear.”

That gives the document a strong appearance of intellectual openness.

And much of that openness was genuine.

But there is a difference between preserving:

> **the clinician's uncertainty**

and preserving:

> **the patient's challenge to whether the clinician had earned that uncertainty through adequate examination.**

The first travels.

The second does not.

So the future reader learns:

> **the neurologist knew the relationship was unclear.**

The future reader does not learn:

> **the patient had directly challenged before discharge that the feet had not been sufficiently dealt with for that uncertainty to be treated as satisfactory.**

That difference is material.

### 6. The result of the admission was selected more strongly than the contest over the result

The final synthesis repeatedly explains why the prolonged admission was useful.

It says the admission allowed many questions to be explored.

It describes the longer stay as having suited the clinicians and patient.

It says the process produced:

> **“firmer footing”**

for the future.

And it seeks to establish:

> **“a clear plan.”**

All of that gives the admission a retrospective shape:

> **difficult case → extensive work → productive clarification → coherent forward plan.**

But the Protest supplies an important competing proposition:

> **the patient believed that one central presenting question remained uncompleted at the moment of discharge.**

That competing proposition is not included.

Thus the document selects **the success-state of the admission** more strongly than **the contest over whether the admission was complete**.

### 7. Later objective information could be added

The production chronology makes the selection problem particularly important.

The documentary object remained capable of receiving information after physical discharge.

The later DaT information was incorporated.

So the document was not simply frozen at the moment the patient walked out.

It was capable of amendment, consolidation and retrospective synthesis.

That means the absence of the Protest cannot be understood merely as:

> **there was no opportunity to put it in.**

There was a post-discharge documentary interval.

Later clinical information entered.

The Protest did not.

The reason for that differential selection is a provenance question.

The fact of differential selection is visible.

### 8. Information supporting the neurological diagnosis was highly portable

The DaT result strengthened the Parkinsonian diagnosis.

It was incorporated into the developing record.

The genetic reasoning was elaborated.

The Parkinsonian phenotype was described extensively.

The treatment strategy was explained in detail.

Those facts all strengthen the coherence of the neurological account.

They travel.

The patient's challenge did something different.

It did not strengthen the diagnosis.

It qualified the **adequacy of the management disposition**.

That information did not travel.

This creates an asymmetry:

> **information strengthening the chosen forward model is made highly portable; information qualifying the completeness of that model is not.**

That is an observable property of the final artefact.

It is not, by itself, proof of motive.

### 9. “Markedly divorced” is a selection from the patient's narrative

The patient's distinction between internal leg tension and painful corns/calluses is preserved in unusually forceful language:

> **“markedly divorced.”**

There was a legitimate phenomenological observation beneath it.

The patient experienced different bodily sensations differently.

But the document selects that observation because it helps explain why the painful structural feet might be considered separately.

The later reader receives:

> **the patient himself distinguished these experiences.**

What the reader does not receive is:

> **the patient later challenged the practical separation of the feet from the neurological inquiry.**

So two patient-generated facts existed.

One supported separation.

One challenged separation.

Only one became part of the durable clinical architecture.

That is particularly significant.

### 10. The structural feet were preserved, but their unresolved relationship was weakened

The feet are not literally omitted.

That is why “the feet disappeared from the record” is an inaccurate description.

Their structural features survive.

Their painful history survives.

Podiatry survives.

The unresolved nature of their relationship survives in:

> **“Not clear.”**

What becomes less portable is the proposition:

> **this unresolved relationship itself remains a central neurological problem requiring active reintegration.**

So the documentary operation is more subtle than deletion.

The facts remain.

The **relationship and priority** change.

### 11. The dystonic lower-limb finding survives without being allowed to reorganise the foot story

Likewise, the dystonic-looking pseudo-footdrop is preserved.

It is not hidden.

That matters greatly.

The final reader can find:

> **possible dystonic posture.**

The reader can also find:

> **structural feet.**

But the document does not force the reader to ask:

> **Are these observations interacting?**

Each fact is individually portable.

The relationship between them is weakly portable.

This is perhaps the defining documentary technique of the case:

> **preserve the facts while weakening the necessity of their connection.**

### 12. The Sinemet reasoning is made exceptionally portable

The treatment-policy reasoning is one of the most elaborated parts of the document.

The reader is told why caution is necessary.

The author concedes that levodopa may work very well.

He concedes possible future regret.

He invokes the patient's youth and risks of dyskinesia.

He records patient understanding.

That policy can therefore survive eight years and still be explained from the original letters.

It was built to travel.

And in 2025, it does.

Monaghan can return to it almost intact.

This shows what high documentary portability looks like.

### 13. Compare that with the Protest

The Protest has virtually zero portability.

Unless the patient personally retells it, it disappears.

It is not in the definitive synthesis.

It is not attached to “Not clear.”

It is not attached to Podiatry.

It is not attached to later medication-responsive foot observations.

It is not available to the GP as part of the formal account.

It is not available to later reviewers who start with the discharge document.

So two pieces of information that originated in the same encounter acquire radically different lifespans:

> **Sinemet-policy reasoning: eight-year documentary durability.**

> **patient challenge to foot incompleteness: dependent upon patient memory and later reconstruction.**

That difference is the practical meaning of documentary selection.

### 14. Selection changes future clinical meaning

The effect is not confined to historical fairness.

Suppose a later clinician knows:

> structural feet + Parkinsonism + “Not clear” + Podiatry.

That clinician can reasonably treat later foot pain as another problem occurring within the established categories.

But suppose the same clinician also knows:

> **before the founding summary was finalised, the patient expressly challenged the treating neurologist that the feet had never been adequately investigated during the admission.**

Now later evidence takes on a different significance.

Procyclidine responsiveness matters more.

Repeated fifth-MTPJ recurrence matters more.

Brennan's reconnection matters more.

OFF-related pain matters more.

Sinemet responsiveness matters more.

So omission changes the evidential weight of future facts.

That is why it is clinically material.

### 15. The document selected an answer-state over a question-state

This may be the simplest way to understand it.

At the bedside after the Protest, the case contained an active question:

> **Have we actually completed the foot component of this neurological admission?**

The final document instead carries an answer-state:

> **feet remain “Not clear,” Podiatry will deal with the structural component, neurological management proceeds.**

The original question is transformed into a management disposition.

That is the central selection.

### 16. The fork itself was left behind

This also means that what disappeared was not just the patient's words.

The Protest created a fork:

> reopen;

or:

> proceed.

The finished record carries:

> **proceed.**

It does not carry:

> **proceed despite an express challenge to the adequacy of the foot disposition.**

That distinction matters enormously.

A later reader sees a decision.

They do not see the choice that made it a contested decision.

The fork has been flattened into a line.

### 17. This makes the document more coherent than the encounter

The actual encounter contained contradiction.

The team had done extensive neurological work.

The patient recognised that the feet remained unresolved.

The treatment strategy was defensible.

The phenotype was incompletely integrated.

The patient could understand the strategy and still contest the foot disposition.

Those things can all be true simultaneously.

The final synthesis reduces that contradiction.

It produces:

> unusual case;

> thoughtful reasoning;

> acknowledged uncertainty;

> intelligent patient;

> clear plan.

The document therefore has **greater narrative coherence than the underlying encounter**.

That is not necessarily evidence of bad faith.

It is exactly what synthesis documents often do.

But when the missing contradiction is clinically material, the coherence becomes evidentially important.

### 18. Documentary selection can eventually change memory

Once the selected representation becomes the authoritative history, it can shape later recollection.

The GP relies on it.

Later clinicians rely on it.

The author himself later relies upon it.

By 2025, when Monaghan says he cannot remember everything and points back to the old letters, the selected record has become the practical memory system.

The information that travelled is easy to retrieve.

The information left behind becomes difficult to recover.

Thus selection at discharge can become **memory architecture eight years later**.

### 19. The complaint reveals the consequences of selection

The 2025 complaint is written without the full documentary history.

The patient knows through his body that something is wrong.

But because the Protest and structural-dynamic question were not carried forward institutionally, he initially frames the challenge around:

> neuropathy;

> anchoring;

> Sinemet;

> failure to reassess.

The rebuttal can then answer from the portable parts of the record:

> the Sinemet policy was reasoned;

> uncertainty remained;

> the old letters show the clinical thinking.

The information that would most strongly qualify those letters—the original challenge to their emerging disposition—is not inside them.

So the complaint itself demonstrates the long-term informational consequence of the 2017 selection.

### 20. What a fair record would have made portable

It did not need to contain every word of the NCT analogy.

It did not need to accuse the treating team of failure.

A fair and clinically useful representation could simply have carried:

> **Patient remains concerned that the painful structural foot problem through which he presented has not been adequately resolved. Although Podiatry input is appropriate, the relationship between the structural feet and the dystonic/Parkinsonian lower-limb phenotype remains an active neurological question.**

That would have preserved both truths:

> the clinicians' management reasoning;

and:

> the unresolved contest.

The subsequent record would then have possessed somewhere for later evidence to land.

<!-- Original source heading: Profile of Documentary Selection -->

> **The July 2017 discharge synthesis did not simply compress the admission; it selected which aspects of the encounter would become portable. It preserved extensive diagnostic reasoning, Parkinsonism, possible PRKN, dystonic-looking gait, structural feet, “Not clear,” Podiatry, detailed Sinemet restraint, the patient's intelligence, his capacity to ask appropriate questions, his understanding of the treatment rationale, the claimed benefit of the prolonged admission, “firmer footing,” a “clear plan” and “As per above.” It did not preserve the patient's direct discharge-day challenge that the feet through which he had presented had not been adequately dealt with. The effect is particularly striking because the document preserves patient-generated information that supports its architecture—such as his phenomenological distinction between leg tension and foot pain—while omitting the patient-generated information that directly challenges the practical separation of those domains. It also remained open after physical discharge and incorporated later diagnostic information, demonstrating that the final artefact was a retrospective synthesis rather than a frozen bedside snapshot. The result was a document more coherent than the encounter: clinician uncertainty and patient understanding became highly portable, while the contest over whether the unresolved feet had been adequately investigated did not. The omitted fact therefore affected not merely historical completeness but the meaning of later evidence, because future clinicians inherited the disposition without inheriting the fact that its adequacy had been challenged before finalisation.**

The irreducible version is:

> **The record did not lose the facts.**
> **It selected which facts were allowed to carry the meaning of the case forward.**

And the sharpest form:

> **It preserved that the patient was an intelligent questioner.**
> **It did not preserve the question that mattered.**

<!-- Original series navigation: Next: Profile 10 — The Patient as the Only Continuous Integrator. -->

# Risk Rank 03 — Original Profile 06

**Severity:** CRITICAL — LOAD-BEARING  
**Primary defendant exposure:** The founding synthesis turns unresolved clinical material into an operational, portable and apparently coherent management state.

## Profile 06 — Profile of the Discharge Summary

<!-- SOURCE: Pasted markdown(20260901-115307).md -->

<!-- Original source heading: Profile of the Discharge Summary -->

### The Document That Should Not Have Been Possible

The July 2017 discharge summary becomes difficult to understand once it is placed beside the people and events that preceded it.

Not because its individual clinical facts are generally false.

Quite the opposite.

Its most troubling characteristic is that **it contains much of the information necessary to see the problem, yet arranges that information in a form that allows the problem to disappear.**

That is what the preceding profiles reveal.

### 1. What had to be true before this document could exist

Before considering a single sentence of the discharge synthesis, four things were already true.

#### The patient

The patient was intelligent, observant and unusually capable of describing his physical state.

He had lived with the developing problem for approximately thirteen years.

He knew his feet.

They had been painful, structurally abnormal and repeatedly treated for years.

He was capable of distinguishing different bodily experiences and capable of understanding complicated neurological explanations.

Neurology itself describes him as highly intelligent, insightful and appropriately questioning.

#### The GP referral

Brennan had not sent Neurology a Parkinson's case with a separate Podiatry problem.

He had sent:

> **feet + gait + tone + toe-clawing + movement-initiation difficulty + deteriorating dexterity + failing function**

under the unresolved question:

> **“?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis.”**

The information state arriving at the university was integrated.

#### The receiving department

The patient arrived in a university Neurology department surrounded by unusually relevant expertise in:

- very-young-onset Parkinsonism;
- PRKN/PARK2 disease;
- dystonia;
- foot dystonia;
- dystonic pseudo-footdrop;
- anticholinergic treatment;
- levodopa responsiveness;
- and the particular dyskinesia problem associated with young patients and parkin disease.

The relevant intellectual territory was not obscure within Galway Neurology.

#### The admission

The hospital then spent eight days investigating the patient intensively.

It found:

- severe, genuine young-onset Parkinsonism;
- an extraordinarily abnormal gait;
- a pseudo-footdrop explicitly considered suggestive of dystonic posture;
- structural foot abnormalities;
- a longstanding painful-foot history;
- possible PRKN disease;
- and sufficient atypicality to justify extensive imaging, blood testing, complex-case discussion and genetic reasoning.

The neurological investigation repeatedly expanded.

The painful feet themselves did not.

After their admission examination they were not returned to.

By discharge, therefore, one proposition should have been impossible to miss:

> **The relationship between the painful structural feet and the dynamic neurological lower-limb syndrome had not been worked out.**

The project reconstruction now states this as the distinction between an uncertainty retained as an **active neurological problem** and an uncertainty parked at the boundary while its structural component is routed elsewhere.

### 2. That is what makes the discharge summary so troubling

The discharge summary did not lack the facts.

It contained them.

It contained Parkinsonism.

It contained dystonia.

It contained the pseudo-footdrop.

It contained structural feet.

It contained the painful-foot history.

It contained possible neuropathy.

It contained PRKN reasoning.

It contained Podiatry.

It contained deliberate levodopa restraint.

It contained the patient's intelligence.

It contained his questions.

It contained extensive discussion.

And it contained:

> **“Not clear.”**

The defect is therefore not principally an absence of raw information.

It is **what the document does to the relationship between the information it contains**.

The current canonical analysis describes the resulting architecture as one in which Parkinsonism and its treatment strategy became highly developed and portable while the unresolved structural-dynamic relationship did not travel forward with equivalent force.

That is exactly what the preceding profiles now make visible.

### 3. The document takes one integrated problem and distributes it

Brennan sent one unresolved problem into hospital.

The discharge summary sends several managed problems back out.

Parkinsonism belongs to Neurology.

Genetics belongs to further specialist investigation.

Treatment belongs to a staged neurological strategy.

Work belongs to Occupational Health and certification.

Rehabilitation belongs to Physiotherapy and Occupational Therapy.

The structural feet belong to Podiatry.

Possible neuropathy remains a possible explanation.

Every component has somewhere to go.

But the relationship between them has no obvious owner.

That is the fundamental transformation.

> **The patient entered as one integrated clinical question and left as a collection of administratively manageable components.**

The case therefore becomes manageable before it becomes fully explanatory.

Document #22 describes precisely this distinction:

> **management completeness became stronger than explanatory completeness.**

That may be the single best description of what this discharge document achieved.

### 4. “Markedly divorced” performs extraordinary work

One of the most consequential passages is the statement that the patient had in his own mind:

> **“markedly divorced”**

the internal sensation of leg tension from the pain associated with corns and calluses.

There is a legitimate clinical observation underneath this.

The patient had noticed that reducing prolonged weight-bearing improved some foot pain while the internal bodily tension persisted.

He had therefore distinguished two experiences.

But a distinction in experience is not a determination of causation.

It does not establish that:

- the neurological disorder had no effect upon foot posture;
- foot posture had no effect upon painful structural lesions;
- dystonia and structural pathology were incapable of interacting;
- toe-clawing was irrelevant to the feet;
- or Podiatry resolved the neurological question.

Yet within the finished architecture, **“markedly divorced” assists the separation**.

The patient himself can appear to supply part of the justification for dividing the problem.

This is especially striking because the same document portrays him as unusually intelligent and insightful.

His intelligence is therefore made evidentially useful when it supports his ability to distinguish symptoms and understand treatment reasoning.

What does not become equally durable is the obvious implication of that same intelligence:

> **he was also capable of recognising when the thing he had come in with had not been dealt with.**

### 5. “Not clear” sounds safer than it is

There is nothing inherently improper about writing:

> **“Not clear.”**

Clinical uncertainty is normal.

The problem is **where the uncertainty is placed and what happens next**.

After this admission, “Not clear” does not function as:

> **relationship unresolved — keep this open neurologically.**

It functions alongside:

> **Podiatry + detailed Parkinsonism strategy + discharge + follow-up.**

That changes its practical meaning.

It becomes uncertainty that does not impede closure.

And the previous profiles make that difficult to justify as mere lack of available information.

The clinicians already knew:

- the feet were structurally abnormal;
- the gait was dystonic-looking;
- toe phenomena existed;
- severe young-onset Parkinsonism existed;
- PRKN was plausible;
- and the feet had not been revisited.

Thus the obvious unanswered question was not:

> **Do we know every mechanism causing every episode of pain?**

No clinician could reasonably be expected to answer that perfectly.

It was:

> **Have we actually partitioned the structural feet from the dynamic neurological foot/lower-limb phenomena sufficiently to send this relationship forward as anything other than an active neurological question?**

The surviving record does not demonstrate that partition.

### 6. Podiatry gives an unresolved question the appearance of an answer

Podiatry was clinically rational.

The patient had genuine structural pathology.

That is not the criticism.

The problem is what the Podiatry destination does within the architecture of the document.

It answers:

> **What is being done about the feet?**

without necessarily answering:

> **What is the neurological relationship of the feet?**

Those are different questions.

Once Podiatry has been assigned, however, the feet possess a destination.

That makes the case easier to close.

The structural pathology can be treated there.

Neurology can continue treating Parkinsonism.

The unresolved relationship between the two no longer needs an obvious institutional owner.

Thus:

> **routing becomes capable of substituting for integration.**

This is one reason the case later appears complicated.

The complexity was not simply inherent in the patient.

Part of it was produced by splitting information that subsequently had to be recombined.

### 7. The extraordinary admission is then used to validate the result

The note says that the prolonged admission:

> **“has all suited us and Liam very well”**

and that it gave the clinicians:

> **“a firmer footing going forward for having a plan.”**

Read without the preceding profiles, this sounds reassuring.

Read after them, it becomes deeply problematic.

The admission was indeed extensive.

But its very extensiveness throws the missing return to the feet into greater relief.

A long admission is not necessarily evidence that every central question was investigated.

Yet the document converts the duration and sophistication of the admission into evidence of confidence:

> **eight days of investigation → firmer footing → coherent plan.**

The more accurate counter-question is:

> **Firmer footing on what?**

Certainly on the existence of Parkinsonism.

Certainly on the seriousness and atypicality of the syndrome.

Certainly on the need for neurological follow-up.

Certainly on the treatment risks faced by a very young patient.

But not, on the surviving evidence, on the relationship between:

> **structural feet + pain + toe phenomena + dystonic-looking lower-limb posture + gait.**

The narrative therefore allows certainty acquired in one part of the case to lend authority to closure in another part that remained unresolved.

### 8. The treatment strategy becomes exceptionally well defended

The contrast is stark.

The decision to delay levodopa and proceed cautiously is extensively reasoned.

The patient's age is discussed.

Dyskinesia risk is discussed.

Impulse-control risk is discussed.

Alternative medication sequencing is discussed.

Senior colleagues are invoked.

Possible future benefit from levodopa is acknowledged.

Even the possibility of later regret is entertained.

This limb of the plan receives intellectual depth.

The feet do not receive equivalent integration.

That asymmetry matters.

The final record therefore communicates:

> **Here is why we are doing what we are doing neurologically.**

But for the original structural-dynamic foot problem the answer remains:

> **Not clear + Podiatry.**

The two propositions occupy radically different evidential depths inside the same document.

### 9. The patient-understanding language then stabilises the plan

The discharge synthesis repeatedly emphasises the patient's:

- intelligence;
- insight;
- retention of information;
- appropriate questioning;
- and understanding of why levodopa or dopamine-agonist treatment was being delayed.

Again, those observations may all be accurate.

But their documentary function matters.

They tell the future reader:

> **this complicated plan was explained to an unusually capable patient and he understood it.**

Understanding, however, is not agreement.

And understanding the reason for delayed levodopa says nothing about whether the patient accepted the adequacy of the foot disposition.

The document therefore carries forward **patient understanding of the chosen management rationale** without carrying forward an equally strong warning that the overall sufficiency of the foot disposition was contested.

That imbalance materially changes how consensual and settled the admission can appear.

The counsel analysis correctly warns that these later-finalised statements are not self-proving contemporaneous facts merely because they appear in the finished artefact.

### 10. “Clear plan” is the decisive contradiction

The note says it exists partly:

> **“to set out for him a clear plan.”**

But clarity of plan is not the same thing as adequacy of explanation.

This document demonstrates exactly how the two can separate.

The plan can be perfectly clear:

- do this medication first;
- introduce this later;
- avoid these drugs for now;
- attend these services;
- see Podiatry;
- follow up with Neurology.

And yet the central relationship can remain unresolved.

The clarity is therefore **operational clarity**.

The clinical relationship remains unclear.

That distinction should have been preserved explicitly.

Instead the language of “clear plan” helps the whole admission acquire a settled appearance.

### 11. “As per above” turns narrative arrangement into future management

The formal Plan/Recommendations does something remarkable.

It says:

> **“As per above.”**

That means the preceding narrative is not merely history.

It becomes the plan.

The selection and arrangement of information in the narrative are thereby converted into an operational forward state.

The document is telling Brennan, the patient and subsequent clinicians:

> **the meaning of the case and the instructions for what happens next are contained in the account you have just read.**

This makes the architecture consequential.

If the narrative makes Parkinsonism richly portable while the mixed foot relationship is left at:

> **Not clear + Podiatry**

then that asymmetry itself becomes part of management.

Document #22 identifies precisely this question: what final case model is the reader expected to inherit when “As per above” incorporates the narrative into the plan?

### 12. The document was still capable of receiving information after the patient left

The finished artefact cannot be treated simply as a bedside note frozen at discharge.

The patient physically left on **11 July**.

The document explicitly contains:

> **“ADDENDUM at Transcription finalisation 2017-07-13th\_21:41hrs”**

and incorporates the DaT scan performed on **13 July**.

Final consultant sign-off occurred on **17 July**.

The document was therefore temporally layered and remained capable of incorporating later information.

This matters because the Protest had already occurred before documentary closure.

It means that the final discharge representation is a **constructed retrospective synthesis of what the admission meant**, not merely an untouched accumulation of bedside facts.

The central forensic question therefore becomes unavoidable:

> **How did the raw information generated by the admission become this particular finished information state?**

### 13. The Protest converts an unresolved defect into a known contested decision

The Protest is central to the evidential status of the discharge architecture.

Before it, the record already contained an integrated GP referral, longstanding painful abnormal feet, severe young-onset Parkinsonism, a dystonic-looking pseudo-footdrop, PRKN under consideration, genuine structural feet, no documented focused return to those feet after the admission examination, and an unresolved structural–dynamic relationship.

The Protest then did something decisive: it placed the treating neurologist on direct notice that the patient regarded that unresolved relationship as an inadequacy in the proposed closure of the admission.

From that moment the information state was no longer merely:

> **the relationship is unresolved.**

It was:

> **the relationship is unresolved, the patient has expressly challenged the adequacy of closing the admission on that basis, and the decision to proceed is now a decision made after notice of the challenge.**

The patient did not create the contradiction.

He identified it before the durable account was finalised.

The later omission therefore does not merely remove a conversation. It removes the visible fork between reopening the unresolved relationship and preserving the existing disposition.

### 14. The Protest should have forced the document to preserve the fork

Put the previous profiles together.

#### The GP had already asked the question.

He sent feet, gait, tone and toe-clawing together.

#### The patient embodied the question.

He had lived with the feet for thirteen years.

#### The department possessed the relevant expertise.

PRKN, dystonia, dystonic feet and young-onset Parkinsonism were unusually close to its clinical and academic environment.

#### The admission generated the relevant findings.

Parkinsonism, possible PRKN, structural feet and dystonic-looking pseudo-footdrop were all present in the hospital's own material.

No one needed the patient to discover those facts.

No one needed the patient to teach Neurology about dystonia.

No one needed the patient to know what PRKN meant.

The obvious medical task was to ask:

> **How do these things relate?**

That is why the existence of the Protest is ultimately less damning than the fact that **a Protest was necessary at all**.

By the last day the patient was forced to reduce an extraordinarily sophisticated neurological admission to an elementary observation:

> **You still haven't dealt with my feet.**

The failure precedes the statement.

The statement merely exposes it.

### 15. The discharge summary then converts that exposed failure into a coherent history

This is the heart of the document.

The patient entered with one problem.

The admission discovered extraordinary neurological disease.

The relationship of that disease to the painful feet remained unresolved.

The absence of a return to the feet left the relationship incompletely investigated.

The patient recognised this.

Yet the forward document manages to say, simultaneously:

> the case is unusual;

> the case is complex;

> extensive work has been done;

> senior expertise has been involved;

> the patient is intelligent;

> the patient understands;

> the feet may be separate;

> perhaps neuropathy;

> **Not clear;**

> Podiatry;

> **firmer footing;**

> **clear plan;**

> **As per above.**

That is the extraordinary achievement of the document.

It does not solve the contradiction.

It **organises around it sufficiently well that the contradiction no longer prevents forward movement.**

### 16. Why the case subsequently looks so complicated

The discharge summary may also explain why the historical case later appears vastly more complicated than the original clinical problem actually was.

Once the document distributes the information:

- Parkinsonism develops its own neurological history;
- Podiatry develops a structural-foot history;
- pain develops possible neuropathic explanations;
- dystonia appears locally;
- medication responses appear at different times;
- GP repeatedly reconnects the domains;
- Movement Disorders later produces local OFF-related interpretations;
- and each subsequent observation finds somewhere to live.

The later investigator then confronts years of seemingly heterogeneous information.

But the originating question was simple:

> **What relationship exists between the neurological lower-limb disorder and these painful structurally abnormal feet?**

The discharge summary does not preserve that question with the same durability with which it preserves its components.

That is how a simple unresolved relationship can become an apparently extraordinarily complicated longitudinal case.

### 17. The document is therefore not merely incomplete

“Incomplete discharge summary” is too weak.

Nor is “wrong diagnosis” accurate.

Nor is “feet omitted” accurate, because the feet are actually present.

The stronger description is:

> **a fact-rich but relationship-poor synthesis which converts an incompletely resolved integrated presentation into a highly coherent management state.**

Or, more starkly:

> **The discharge summary contains almost everything required to see the problem, but arranges those facts so that no one proposition requires the problem to remain open.**

That is why it became so durable.

Every later reader could find something true in it.

Parkinsonism was true.

Structural feet were true.

Dystonic-looking gait was true.

Treatment caution had a real rationale.

Podiatry had a legitimate role.

Uncertainty was genuinely present.

The document's strength comes from the truth of its components.

Its vulnerability lies in their arrangement.

### 18. The profile in one paragraph

> **The July 2017 discharge summary is a highly sophisticated, fact-rich and plan-setting document produced after an eight-day university Neurology admission of an intelligent 39-year-old referred with one integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–function syndrome. The admission established severe very-young-onset Parkinsonism, entertained PRKN, recognised a dystonic-looking pseudo-footdrop and recorded genuine structural foot disease, yet did not return after the admission examination to resolve the relationship between those findings and the longstanding painful feet. The final synthesis nevertheless distributed the components into workable destinations: Parkinsonism and its elaborate treatment strategy remained with Neurology; structural feet went to Podiatry; possible neuropathy remained available; the unresolved relationship became “Not clear”; the patient's phenomenological distinction became “markedly divorced”; the prolonged admission supplied “firmer footing”; the intelligent patient was represented as understanding the treatment rationale; a “clear plan” emerged; and “As per above” converted that narrative into the formal management state. The Protest did not create the underlying failure. The preceding patient, GP, departmental and admission profiles show that the integrated problem should already have been unavoidable. The deeper indictment is therefore that a Protest was necessary at all—and that after the patient explicitly restored the obvious unresolved relationship on the final day, the durable document still carried forward the management arrangement more successfully than it carried forward the unresolved question.**

### The shortest version

> **The discharge summary is damning not because it lacks the facts, but because it contains the facts and still manages to lose the question.**

And perhaps the sentence immediately beneath it should be:

> **Everything we now know about the patient, his GP referral, the expertise of the receiving department and the conduct of the eight-day admission says that this information state should never have been possible.**
