# Risk Rank 26 — Original Profile 23

**Severity:** SYNTHESIS / EVIDENTIAL CONTROL / SECONDARY  
**Primary defendant exposure:** Separates what the record directly establishes from propositions requiring expert, provenance, causation or intent evidence.

## Profile 23 — Proof Strength

<!-- SOURCE: Pasted markdown (2)(4).md; source text preserved in full below. -->

<!-- Original series label: Profile 12 of 12 — Proof Strength -->

### What Is Established, What Is Inference, and What Still Requires External Proof

The case is now strong enough that its propositions should no longer all be spoken in the same evidential voice.

Some things are documentary facts.

Some are fixed longitudinal facts produced by several records together.

Some are strong inferences from those facts.

Some require clinical expert evidence.

Some require provenance or version evidence before they can safely be alleged.

That distinction does not weaken the case.

It protects it.

Because the central architecture does **not** depend upon proving the most ambitious propositions.

A very substantial case remains even if every proposition requiring expert evidence, motive evidence or drafting-history evidence is temporarily removed.

### 1. The first tier — Direct documentary facts

These are the safest propositions in the case.

They do not require an expert to interpret what happened.

The primary record establishes that the GP sent Neurology an integrated presentation involving:

> feet;

> abnormal gait;

> pathological tone;

> toe-clawing;

> movement-initiation difficulty;

> deteriorating dexterity and function;

> and an open question of Parkinsonism or another underlying diagnosis.

The Neurology admission then establishes:

> severe young-onset Parkinsonism;

> a strikingly abnormal gait;

> an apparent footdrop phenomenon specifically described as potentially dystonic and **not true footdrop**;

> structural foot abnormalities;

> a longstanding painful foot history;

> and possible PRKN/PARK2.

The discharge record itself states that the relationship of the painful feet to the neurological picture was:

> **“Not clear.”**

Those are foundational facts.

### 2. The foot-examination chronology is factual

For project purposes, the admission record establishes the operative examination sequence:

> **the feet were examined on the first day; they were not returned to during the remaining admission.**

That fact does not require an expert conclusion about what should have happened.

Its significance can be argued separately.

But the sequence itself is central:

> initial foot examination
> → neurological case expands dramatically
> → dystonic-looking lower-limb phenomenon recognised
> → no return to the feet
> → discharge.

That chronology should always be kept distinct from any later opinion about adequacy.

### 3. The Protest is an event, not a hypothesis in the reconstruction

Within the project's established evidential reconstruction, the discharge-day Protest is a fixed event.

The patient recognised that the presenting feet had not been returned to.

He caused Monaghan to be summoned.

He used the NCT/bald-tyres analogy to challenge the completeness of the admission.

Monaghan responded.

The patient left.

The important factual effect is:

> **before the durable discharge representation was finally settled, the treating neurologist had been directly put on notice that the patient regarded the unresolved feet as an incompleteness in the admission.**

The Protest therefore establishes **notice and contest**.

It does not, by itself, prove negligence, dishonesty or motive.

Those are separate propositions.

### 4. The documentary chronology is also factual

The surviving evidence establishes that the documentary object was not simply completed at the instant of physical discharge.

There was document activity before discharge.

The patient physically left on 11 July.

The DaT scan occurred on 13 July.

Post-discharge material was incorporated.

The GP was still waiting for formal correspondence.

The surviving summary was finally consultant-confirmed later.

Therefore the safe proposition is:

> **The Protest occurred before the final durable representation of the admission had completed its production and adoption process.**

That is strong.

A stronger proposition—

> **the Protest caused the document to be delayed**

—is not presently proved.

Nor is:

> **particular language was drafted in response to the Protest.**

Those require provenance evidence.

### 5. The left fifth MTPJ continuity is factual

The anatomy does not need to be treated as an uncertain expert reconstruction.

The patient identifies the longstanding outside-left focal corn/pain.

The Podiatry sequence repeatedly identifies that same enduring focal problem at the:

> **left fifth MTPJ.**

Across eight out of eight recorded Podiatry treatment visits, that site is treated.

This establishes:

> **a persistent anatomically stable structural lesion in the same focal territory.**

It is entirely proper to say:

> Podiatry repeatedly treated the left fifth MTPJ.

There is no need to dilute that into:

> “somewhere around the outside of the foot.”

### 6. Neurological treatment-state modification is factual

Separately, the longitudinal record establishes that neurological treatment state affected the feet/lower-limb territory.

The record contains:

> major procyclidine benefit;

> deterioration when procyclidine was withdrawn;

> feet becoming restless;

> gait falling apart;

> rapid return of the outside-left-foot phenomenon;

> later foot cramping with increased tone;

> later OFF-related pain and shuffling;

> improvement of the feet when Sinemet reaches the system;

> and profound relief after Sinemet CR.

These are treatment-state observations.

The safe conclusion is:

> **the clinical state of the painful feet/lower limbs was repeatedly neurologically modifiable.**

That does not require proof that the structural lesion itself was neurologically caused.

### 7. The combined fifth-MTPJ proposition is also strongly established

Once those two factual streams are placed together:

> repeated structural failure at the left fifth MTPJ;

and:

> medication-dependent change in that same focal territory;

we can safely state the central relationship:

> **a genuine structural fifth-MTPJ problem had a clinical expression that was responsive to neurological treatment state.**

That is substantially different from saying:

> Parkinsonism caused the corn.

The former is supported.

The latter requires further mechanistic evidence.

That distinction should remain permanent.

### 8. The GP's longitudinal reconstruction is factual

Brennan repeatedly reconnects the domains.

He refers the integrated phenotype.

Later he records foot cramping with increased tone.

He describes the feet in relation to Parkinsonian gait and tone.

During the 2023 crisis he explicitly says the severe foot pain is likely contributed to by Parkinsonian tone and recognises it as the patient's original presentation returning.

Therefore it is factual to say:

> **primary care repeatedly regenerated the relationship that the founding specialist account had left unresolved.**

Whether Brennan's causal interpretation was medically correct in every detail remains clinical opinion.

But that he made the connection is documentary fact.

### 9. The continued uncertainty in 2025 is factual

The 2017 record says:

> **“Not clear.”**

The 2025 rebuttal still uses conditional language:

> **“If there is a degree of peripheral neuropathy here…”**

At the same time Monaghan states that there has been:

> **a clear understanding for quite some time that Sinemet helps the pain.**

That juxtaposition is documentary fact.

It supports the strong longitudinal observation:

> **neurological treatment responsiveness became increasingly clear while the peripheral explanatory proposition remained conditional.**

No accusation is needed to make that point.

### 10. The author's later ownership of the 2017 record is factual

In 2025 Monaghan points the reviewer to the historical letters and says his clinical reasoning should be evident from them.

That establishes an important evidential fact:

> **the author later adopted the 2017 documentary record as an explanation of his historical reasoning.**

This permits the case to examine the record not merely as an old medical note but as an artefact later relied upon in defence of the historical management.

That is a major change in evidential status.

### 11. The record's incompleteness in relation to the Protest is factual

We know:

> the Protest occurred;

and:

> the final synthesis does not preserve it.

Therefore we do not need to speculate that the record is incomplete in every respect.

We can state precisely:

> **the final durable representation is incomplete with respect to one material event known to its author: the patient's direct challenge to the adequacy of the foot disposition.**

That proposition is enough to defeat any assumption that the discharge summary can automatically be treated as the complete reality of the encounter.

It does not make the document worthless.

It limits what the document can prove about itself.

### 12. The second tier — Strong structural inferences

The next propositions are not literal quotations from a single document.

They arise from the arrangement of several established facts.

These are still strong.

The first is:

> **recognition without integration.**

Neurology recognised:

> structural feet;

> dystonic-looking lower limb;

> young-onset Parkinsonism;

> possible PRKN.

But the relationship remained “Not clear,” and the feet were not returned to during the admission.

It is therefore fair to characterise the admission as:

> **recognising the relevant components without completing their integration.**

That is a synthesis, not an invented fact.

### 13. “Management completeness exceeded explanatory completeness” is a strong inference

The final record contains a highly developed management strategy.

It contains:

> treatment sequencing;

> genetic thinking;

> rehabilitation;

> Podiatry;

> patient counselling;

> a clear plan.

Yet one central relationship remains unresolved.

So it is reasonable to conclude:

> **the document achieved a higher degree of management closure than explanatory closure.**

That is a structural reading of the artefact.

It does not require alleging bad intent.

### 14. “The relationship had no durable owner” is a strong inference

The records show:

> Neurology managed Parkinsonism;

> Podiatry managed structural lesions;

> GP managed continuity;

> Movement Disorders later managed OFF state.

There is no corresponding durable assignment:

> **this clinician/service owns the interaction between neurological lower-limb state and structural foot pathology.**

Thus:

> **the relationship was effectively orphaned**

is a strong systems inference.

It should not be confused with saying no clinician ever thought about it.

The stronger and safer point is:

> **no durable ownership of the relationship is visible in the longitudinal management architecture.**

### 15. The cross-service information split is a strong inference

Podiatry repeatedly knows:

> where the structural lesion is and that it recurs.

Neurology knows:

> the foot territory changes with neurological state.

There is no visible durable synthesis of those streams.

Thus:

> **the information required to understand the relationship became distributed between services**

is strongly supported.

Again, formal conclusions about data-controller responsibility belong to a different legal/provenance analysis.

The clinical information split itself is visible.

### 16. “Local update without global reconstruction” is one of the strongest longitudinal inferences

The case repeatedly shows new information being acted upon locally:

> Podiatry treats recurrence;

> Neurology adjusts medication;

> Movement Disorders treats OFF state;

> GP escalates severe pain;

> pregabalin is adjusted;

> Sinemet CR is introduced.

Yet no identifiable point replaces the founding feet relationship with a new integrated model.

Therefore:

> **the record repeatedly updates locally without globally reconstructing the original relationship**

is a highly defensible synthesis.

It is perhaps the most important systems proposition in the case.

### 17. “The founding record became self-validating” is a strong evidential inference

The sequence is:

> author creates detailed historical account;

> account becomes authoritative;

> later care inherits it;

> patient challenges history;

> author points reviewer back to the same record as explanation of reasoning.

From that sequence it is fair to say:

> **the record acquired a self-validating function.**

The phrase describes structure.

It does not require proving that anyone deliberately designed the record to protect themselves.

That distinction is essential.

### 18. The patient as continuous integrator is a strong longitudinal inference

The services each hold fragments.

The patient experiences:

> structure;

> pain;

> medication effects;

> gait;

> OFF states;

> treatment responses;

> Protest.

Thus:

> **the patient was the only guaranteed continuous integrator of the whole phenotype**

is well supported.

It does not mean every recollection is automatically correct.

It means only the patient necessarily occupied every part of the longitudinal clinical reality.

### 19. The third tier — Clinical propositions requiring expert evidence

Now we reach propositions that should not be asserted as settled medical causation without expert support.

The most important is the detailed biomechanical chain:

> dystonic/Parkinsonian lower-limb state
> → altered foot position or gait
> → altered lateral forefoot loading
> → increased stress at the vulnerable fifth MTPJ
> → focal pain/callus exacerbation
> → compensatory unloading
> → redistribution through the remaining foot.

This is an eminently plausible hypothesis from the record.

But the detailed causal biomechanics require appropriate expert evidence.

The documents establish why the question should be asked.

They do not, by themselves, prove every link.

### 20. Whether the July 2017 care fell below an appropriate clinical standard requires expert opinion

The record can establish:

> what was known;

> what was examined;

> what was not returned to;

> what uncertainty was recorded;

> what happened after the Protest.

But the proposition:

> **a competent neurologist or movement-disorders service was required, according to the applicable standard, to perform X, Y or Z before discharge**

is a professional-standard conclusion.

That should come from expert evidence.

The architecture can make the question unavoidable.

It should not impersonate the expert who answers it.

### 21. Causation of later harm also requires expert analysis

The longitudinal record strongly supports that failure to integrate the relationship had informational and management consequences.

But stronger medico-legal causation claims require care.

For example:

> would earlier reintegration probably have reduced the 2023 pain crisis?

> would a different treatment plan have prevented pregabalin escalation?

> would earlier levodopa have improved long-term outcome?

> would different foot mechanics management have prevented structural recurrence?

Those are counterfactual medical questions.

They require expert support.

The case does not need to overclaim them in order to remain serious.

### 22. The Sinemet policy should not be treated as proven negligent

Indeed, the record contains substantial reasons why caution was rational:

> young age;

> concern about dyskinesia;

> desire to preserve long-term therapeutic options;

> later emergence of the very dyskinesia feared.

So the safe position is:

> **the levodopa restraint strategy may have been clinically defensible even if the foot phenotype was incompletely integrated.**

That distinction strengthens the case.

It prevents the criticism from collapsing into:

> they should simply have started Sinemet earlier.

The deeper allegation survives even if the Sinemet policy is conceded.

### 23. The fourth tier — Provenance propositions requiring production evidence

These are exceptionally important, but they must remain questions until the documentary production history is obtained.

For example:

> When exactly was “markedly divorced” drafted?

> When did “firmer footing” enter the document?

> When did “clear plan” enter?

> When was the patient's exceptional insight language drafted?

> When was “good understanding” recorded?

> When did “As per above” become the formal plan?

> What existed before the Protest?

> What changed afterward?

> Were drafts retained?

> Was there an earlier discharge version?

These questions go directly to the formation of the durable information object.

But without version history, audit trails, metadata, drafts or system provenance, the case should not claim to know the answer.

### 24. Differential incorporation is factual; the reason for it is not yet proved

We can say:

> **post-discharge diagnostic information was incorporated into the developing document while the Protest was not.**

That is an observable differential.

We cannot yet safely say:

> **the Protest was deliberately excluded because it threatened the management narrative.**

That is an intent claim.

It requires more.

The current evidence permits the question.

It does not yet provide final proof of motive.

### 25. The fifth tier — Intent and concealment

This must be the most carefully handled category.

There are facts capable of supporting concern:

> Monaghan personally knew of the Protest;

> the final document omitted it;

> the document contained extensive coherence-producing language;

> it later became the historical defence;

> the author relied upon it in 2025.

But those facts alone do not establish:

> deliberate concealment;

> dishonest drafting;

> conscious manipulation of the record;

> intention to mislead later carers;

> intention to protect professional reputation.

Those are serious state-of-mind allegations.

They require stronger evidential foundations.

The current case should therefore distinguish:

#### Effect

The omission had the effect of making the final account more coherent and less visibly contested.

#### Possible inference

That differential may justify investigation of how and why the final document was produced.

#### Proven intent

Not established merely by the effect.

This distinction is critical.

### 26. The beauty of the case is that motive is not necessary

This may be the most important evidential point of all.

Suppose, for the sake of argument, that:

> no one acted dishonestly;

> no one deliberately hid anything;

> the Sinemet strategy was clinically defensible;

> Podiatry care was appropriate;

> later doctors acted reasonably within the information available to them.

The central architecture still remains.

One integrated problem entered Neurology.

The components were recognised.

The feet were examined once and not returned to.

The patient protested.

The final durable synthesis omitted the Protest and left the relationship “Not clear” while allocating the components.

Later structural and neurological information accumulated separately.

The same focal territory proved neurologically modifiable.

No durable global reconstruction replaced the founding state.

The author later pointed back to that state as the explanation of historical reasoning.

That case does not depend on proving a bad actor.

It depends on proving an information failure.

And much of that is already documentary.

### 27. The allegation should therefore be built from the lowest necessary rung

A robust allegation should always use the least ambitious proposition capable of carrying the point.

Not:

> **They deliberately concealed the Protest.**

Unless evidence later proves that.

But:

> **The Protest was known to the treating neurologist and absent from the final durable synthesis, with the effect that later readers could not know from that document that the adequacy of the foot disposition had been expressly contested before finalisation.**

Not:

> **Parkinsonian dystonia caused the corn.**

But:

> **The same persistent structural fifth-MTPJ territory was repeatedly shown to be clinically modifiable by neurological treatment state, making its structural-dynamic relationship clinically material.**

Not:

> **They negligently failed to start levodopa.**

But:

> **Even if the levodopa restraint strategy was defensible, that did not resolve or remove the obligation to preserve the unresolved foot phenotype in an updateable form.**

Not:

> **Every later doctor ignored the truth.**

But:

> **Later clinicians repeatedly generated locally useful information without an identified global reconstruction of the original relationship.**

That is how the case becomes hard to dislodge.

### 28. Proof of effect is already considerably stronger than proof of intention

We can already see the documentary effect.

The final synthesis presents:

> a coherent diagnostic narrative;

> extensive reasoning;

> an intelligent and understanding patient;

> a clear plan;

> unresolved feet allocated to Podiatry.

The Protest is absent.

Later readers inherit the selected state.

Later evidence is absorbed locally.

In 2025 the same document is relied upon as historical explanation.

That effect is visible whether or not anyone intended it.

This should be the evidential centre of gravity.

### 29. The project should maintain three different vocabularies

Internally, the project can continue reconstructing mechanisms and testing harder hypotheses.

For expert instruction, the language should become:

> **These facts raise the following clinical questions.**

For counsel or regulator, the safest form is:

> **These documentary facts support this inference; this further proposition requires expert/provenance evidence.**

Keeping those layers separate prevents the strongest evidence from being contaminated by the most contestable allegation.

### 30. The proof matrix of the whole case

At its cleanest, the architecture now looks like this:

#### Established

One integrated referral.

#### Established

Severe young-onset Parkinsonism, dystonic-looking lower limb and structural painful feet identified.

#### Established

One focused foot examination, no return during the eight-day admission.

#### Established

Relationship recorded as “Not clear.”

#### Established

Patient directly protested the unresolved feet before the final documentary process concluded.

#### Established

Final synthesis omitted the Protest and transmitted “Not clear + Podiatry + clear plan.”

#### Established

Same left fifth MTPJ repeatedly treated structurally.

#### Established

Same foot/lower-limb territory repeatedly changed with neurological treatment state.

#### Established

GP repeatedly regenerated the relationship.

#### Established

OFF-related pain and later Sinemet responsiveness entered specialist care.

#### Established

Sinemet CR produced profound relief.

#### Established

Peripheral explanation remained conditional.

#### Established

Author later relied upon the 2017 letters to explain his historical reasoning.

#### Strong inference

Recognition occurred without adequate visible integration.

#### Strong inference

The structural-dynamic relationship acquired no durable owner.

#### Strong inference

Relevant information became divided across services.

#### Strong inference

Later care repeatedly updated locally without global reconstruction.

#### Strong inference

The founding record acquired self-validating authority.

#### Expert question

What was the likely clinical/biomechanical relationship?

#### Expert question

What should competent neurological practice have done?

#### Expert question

What harm probably resulted from failure to integrate?

#### Provenance question

How was the final document constructed before and after the Protest?

#### Intent question

Why was the Protest omitted, and was that omission deliberate?

That hierarchy should remain intact.

<!-- Original source heading: Profile of Proof Strength -->

> **The central case is already substantially documentary and does not depend upon proving its most ambitious propositions. It is established that one integrated feet–gait–tone presentation entered Neurology; that severe young-onset Parkinsonism, a dystonic-looking lower-limb phenomenon and genuine structural painful feet were identified; that the feet were examined once and not returned to during the eight-day admission; that their relationship was recorded as “Not clear”; that the patient directly protested the unresolved feet before the final durable representation was completed; and that the final synthesis transmitted “Not clear,” Podiatry and a coherent management plan without preserving that contest. It is further established that the same left fifth-MTPJ site was repeatedly treated structurally, while the same foot/lower-limb territory repeatedly changed with neurological treatment state; that primary care repeatedly reconnected feet, gait and tone; that later Movement Disorders evidence identified OFF-related pain; that Sinemet was known to improve the feet; and that Sinemet CR produced profound relief while a peripheral neuropathy proposition remained conditional. These facts strongly support structural inferences of recognition without integration, missing ownership, cross-service information separation, repeated local updating without global reconstruction and increasing self-validating authority of the founding record. The detailed biomechanical mechanism, the applicable clinical standard, counterfactual harm and stronger causation propositions require expert evidence. The timing and evolution of specific discharge-summary language require production/version evidence. Deliberate concealment or dishonest drafting require still stronger proof of intent. The allegation therefore becomes strongest when built from the lowest evidential rung necessary: document the information-state failure first, prove clinical causation and provenance separately, and do not make the core case depend upon motive.**

The irreducible version is:

> **We do not need to prove why they did it before we can prove what the information system did.**

And the protective rule for the whole project:

> **Fact first.**
> **Relationship second.**
> **Inference third.**
> **Expert causation after that.**
> **Intent last.**

That completes the original **12-profile collection**.

# Risk Rank 27 — Original Profile 35

**Severity:** SYNTHESIS / EVIDENTIAL CONTROL / SECONDARY  
**Primary defendant exposure:** Synthesises the whole case into one longitudinal theory of unresolved relationship, portability and failed reconstruction.

## Profile 35 — Profile of the Case

<!-- SOURCE: Pasted markdown(20260901-130409).md -->

<!-- Original source heading: Profile of the Case -->

This is not fundamentally a case about a disputed Parkinson's diagnosis.

It is not fundamentally a case about Podiatry.

It is not fundamentally a case about delayed levodopa.

It is not fundamentally a case about one omitted Protest.

It is a case about **an obvious integrated clinical problem being broken into manageable parts without its central relationship ever being resolved, and about the consequences of allowing that arrangement to become the durable history of the patient.**

### The patient who entered hospital

In July 2017, a 39-year-old working man entered a university Neurology department after approximately thirteen years of progressive abnormal bodily tension, gait dysfunction and painful abnormal feet.

He was not confused about what had brought him into medical care.

His feet had been painful and structurally abnormal for years.

He knew them intimately.

He was observant, articulate and capable of distinguishing different bodily sensations.

His own neurologist would describe him as unusually intelligent, insightful and capable of asking appropriate questions.

He did not enter hospital claiming to know his neurological diagnosis.

He entered wanting an explanation for what was happening to him.

### The problem the GP actually sent

The GP did not send a Parkinson's patient with an incidental foot complaint.

He sent one unresolved syndrome:

> **feet + gait + pathological tone + toe-clawing + impaired movement initiation + deteriorating dexterity + failing function.**

The question was open.

Something neurological was clearly happening, but the relationship between the feet, the gait and the abnormal tone had not been divided in advance.

The referral therefore arrived at Neurology in an integrated state.

That is the proper starting point of the case.

### The department that received him

The receiving service was not a general environment unfamiliar with unusual movement disorders.

It was a university Neurology department with unusually close expertise in:

- very-young-onset Parkinsonism;
- PRKN/parkin disease;
- dystonia;
- dystonic feet;
- pseudo-footdrop;
- procyclidine treatment;
- levodopa responsiveness;
- and the problem of early dyskinesia in young patients.

The relevant intellectual territory was already inside the department and its immediate specialist network.

That matters because the later problem was not dependent upon an obscure diagnosis that nobody could reasonably have considered.

The necessary concepts were already available.

### What happened during the eight-day admission

Neurology did a great deal.

It correctly recognised severe, genuine very-young-onset Parkinsonism.

It recognised that the gait was extraordinary.

It described a lower-limb phenomenon resembling footdrop but considered suggestive of dystonic posture.

It considered PRKN.

It documented structural abnormalities of the feet.

It reconstructed the long history.

It videoed the neurological examination.

It performed extensive imaging and laboratory investigation.

It discussed the patient as complex.

It sought senior expertise.

It built a detailed future treatment strategy.

But after the first-day examination, the painful abnormal feet themselves were not examined again.

The investigation expanded around them.

It did not return to them.

That is the first decisive fact of the case.

By the end of eight days, Neurology knew:

> **Parkinsonism was real.**

It knew:

> **the lower limbs contained possible dystonic phenomenology.**

It knew:

> **the feet were structurally abnormal and historically painful.**

What it had not established was:

> **which parts of the foot/lower-limb problem were structural, which were dynamically neurological, and how those processes interacted.**

That relationship remained unresolved.

### Why the Protest became necessary

The most revealing thing about the Protest is not merely that it occurred.

It is that it had to occur.

After eight days of sophisticated neurological investigation, the patient was required to tell the treating neurologist something elementary:

> **You have not dealt with the feet I came in with.**

He did not discover PRKN.

He did not diagnose dystonia.

He did not demand levodopa.

He did not reject Parkinsonism.

He simply recognised that the hospital had performed an extraordinary investigation while failing to return to the part of the presentation that had remained unresolved.

The Protest was therefore not the source of the problem.

It was the patient's real-time detection of the problem.

It proved that the asymmetry in the admission was already visible before he left.

### The discharge summary

The discharge summary is the central documentary object because it does not simply lack information.

It contains almost all the relevant information.

It contains Parkinsonism.

It contains dystonic-looking gait.

It contains structural feet.

It contains pain.

It contains PRKN reasoning.

It contains possible neuropathy.

It contains Podiatry.

It contains the treatment strategy.

It contains the patient's intelligence and understanding.

It contains uncertainty.

And it contains:

> **“Not clear.”**

The problem lies in the arrangement.

The patient entered with one unresolved clinical relationship.

The document leaves with multiple manageable components.

Parkinsonism belongs to Neurology.

Structural feet belong to Podiatry.

Possible neuropathy remains available.

Treatment belongs to a detailed neurological strategy.

The unresolved relationship between those components is left without equivalent ownership.

The plan becomes operationally complete before the explanation becomes clinically complete.

That is the transformation.

### How the summary achieves closure

The document does not falsely claim that every question is answered.

It does something more subtle.

It makes the unresolved state compatible with moving forward.

The patient's distinction between leg tension and foot pain becomes **“markedly divorced.”**

The unresolved relationship becomes:

> **“Not clear.”**

The structural feet receive a destination:

> **Podiatry.**

The lengthy admission is represented as providing:

> **“firmer footing.”**

The patient is represented as intelligent and understanding the treatment rationale.

The document describes a:

> **“clear plan.”**

And finally:

> **“As per above”**

turns the narrative itself into the formal management plan.

Nothing need be blatantly false for the overall information state to become misleadingly settled.

That is what makes the document so durable.

Its individual components are largely true.

Its weakness lies in what their arrangement permits the reader to stop asking.

### The Protest does not create the defect

The case no longer depends upon saying:

> the Protest happened, therefore the discharge summary was defective.

The preceding profiles make the defect visible without it.

The patient profile says the problem should have remained obvious.

The GP profile says the problem entered hospital integrated.

The departmental profile says the necessary expertise was available.

The admission profile says the relevant neurological findings were actually observed.

The fact that the feet were not returned to after day one says the relationship was never completed.

The Protest merely confirms that, before closure, the patient himself recognised exactly that.

The real indictment is:

> **the Protest should have been unnecessary.**

### What happened next

The case then became longitudinal.

And importantly, it initially got much better.

Early treatment produced dramatic improvement.

The patient regained movement and function.

At one point he described himself as:

> **“living in a miracle.”**

That early recovery is critical because it demonstrates how neurologically modifiable the disabling state could be.

But the improvement was difficult to sustain.

Effective treatment repeatedly encountered ceilings.

Procyclidine helped enormously but produced visual and cognitive problems.

Withdrawal made the feet restless and the gait deteriorate.

Foot phenomena returned rapidly with worsening neurological state.

Bilateral foot cramping later returned with increased tone.

Neupro improved mobility but produced behavioural and dyskinetic problems.

Amantadine failed and produced troubling mood effects.

Levodopa eventually arrived but dyskinesia appeared early.

The service's own description eventually became:

> **“an extremely, extremely difficult tight rope.”**

The patient did not simply fail to respond to treatment.

He responded repeatedly.

The problem became sustaining enough treatment without creating another form of disability.

### Why the later course was increasingly predictable

The exact future could not have been known in 2017.

But its architecture became progressively foreseeable.

From the beginning there was:

> **young age + possible PRKN + dystonic-looking lower-limb phenotype + expected treatment responsiveness + anticipated dyskinesia risk.**

Then treatment demonstrated dramatic responsiveness.

Then withdrawal demonstrated that feet and gait could deteriorate together.

Then bilateral foot cramping returned with increasing tone.

Then the GP explicitly connected clawing feet and pain to Parkinsonian tone and gait.

Then levodopa produced the very dyskinesia ceiling Neurology had feared from the beginning.

The service had correctly predicted that effective dopaminergic treatment would eventually become difficult to increase.

What it had not preserved with equal force was the unresolved foot/lower-limb problem that would become increasingly important once that therapeutic window narrowed.

That is the predictability problem.

### The GP over eight years

Primary care provides the longitudinal control.

The same GP who originally sent the integrated syndrome into Neurology repeatedly regenerated the relationship afterward.

He saw:

> **gait + feet + muscle tension**

before diagnosis.

Later:

> **bilateral foot cramping + increased tone.**

Later:

> **clawing feet and pain related to abnormal Parkinsonian tone and gait.**

And in 2023, during catastrophic bilateral foot pain, he explicitly recognised:

> **this was actually the patient's original presentation six years earlier.**

Primary care therefore did not forget the integrated problem.

It repeatedly rediscovered it.

But the GP did not possess the specialist authority to redesign the neurological management architecture.

By 2025 he said so directly: adjusting levodopa without specialist guidance was beyond ordinary GP scope.

Thus the clinician with the longest continuous view could repeatedly see the relationship while lacking the authority to perform the global neurological reconstruction.

### How severe the case became

The later severity was extreme.

By March 2023 the patient was describing his feet as literally killing him and saying that he was losing the will to live.

The GP recorded a:

> **“death wish”**

and:

> **“I am worried about him.”**

The Parkinson's service recorded debilitating pain and:

> **“no quality of life.”**

Pregabalin escalated to 600 mg/day.

It helped, but did not resolve the whole problem.

The patient became frightened of reducing it because of the possibility of the catastrophic pain returning.

Dopaminergic treatment could help movement and pain, but dyskinesia prevented simple escalation.

By 2024 the patient's life was described as:

> **“a lot smaller.”**

By March 2025:

> **“murder in the feet.”**

> **“the first 150 footsteps are torture.”**

> **“I won't live with it.”**

These are not retrospective descriptions manufactured after the dispute.

They belong to the clinical course.

### The final contradiction

By 2025 Neurology already knew that Sinemet improved the feet.

It expected that more Sinemet would probably provide more pain relief.

But more Sinemet would worsen dyskinesia.

Then controlled-release Sinemet produced:

> **“profound relief.”**

That does not prove that every historical foot symptom had one neurological cause.

It does something more important.

It demonstrates the mature version of the unresolved relationship:

> **the same anatomical and functional territory that had been difficult to integrate in 2017 was materially sensitive to dopaminergic treatment, but the treatment capable of helping it had become constrained by the very dyskinesia problem anticipated at the beginning.**

The treatment strategy and the unresolved foot problem had finally collided.

### What the eight days converted the next eight years into

The eight-day admission did not cause the disease.

It did not cause every later symptom.

It did not cause every adverse drug effect.

It did not cause the natural progression of Parkinsonism.

But it did establish the durable information and management state through which those later developments would be understood.

The patient entered with:

> **one integrated question.**

He emerged into:

> **parallel clinical territories.**

The following eight years then repeatedly produced information crossing those territories.

The feet changed with neurological treatment state.

The GP reconnected feet and tone.

Movement Disorders later recognised OFF-related pain and shuffling.

Pregabalin separated some pain from persistent toe curling.

Levodopa changed pain and function.

Yet each observation could be absorbed locally without forcing a return to the original question.

The result was that the patient spent eight years **living the unresolved relationship** rather than having that unresolved relationship carried forward institutionally.

### The human profile of the case

The deepest feature of the case may be the progressive transfer of burden onto the patient.

In 2017 he entered hospital seeking an explanation.

Over time he had to become:

- observer of medication states;
- interpreter of his feet;
- reconciler of prescriptions;
- messenger between Neurology, GP and pharmacy;
- tracker of dose effects;
- recorder of OFF states;
- complainant;
- records requester;
- chronology builder;
- and eventually investigator of the admission itself.

His body repeatedly generated the information.

His GP repeatedly regenerated the relationship.

Specialists repeatedly recognised individual pieces.

But eventually the burden of reconstructing the whole fell upon the person who had already said, on the last day of the original admission:

> **you still have not dealt with my feet.**

### The profile of the case

This is therefore a case with a very small centre and a very large documentary wake.

> **An intelligent 39-year-old man was referred to a university Neurology department with one integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–function syndrome. The department correctly identified severe very-young-onset Parkinsonism, recognised dystonic-looking lower-limb phenomenology, considered PRKN and documented genuine structural foot disease. It investigated the neurological syndrome intensively for eight days but did not return after the first day to the painful feet themselves, leaving their structural–dynamic relationship unresolved. The patient recognised that obvious incompleteness and protested on the final day. The later discharge synthesis nevertheless converted the admission into a coherent forward state: Parkinsonism and its elaborate treatment strategy travelled strongly; the structural feet went to Podiatry; the relationship remained “Not clear”; and narrative coherence, patient-understanding language, “firmer footing,” a “clear plan” and “As per above” made the unresolved state operationally complete. Over the next eight years the patient repeatedly generated evidence that crossed the original boundary: dramatic neurological recovery, medication-dependent foot changes, recurrent foot cramping with tone, GP reconnection of feet and Parkinsonian gait, a narrowing therapeutic window, dyskinesia, catastrophic bilateral foot pain, maximal pregabalin, OFF-related pain, shrinking quality of life and finally profound relief when dopaminergic coverage was changed. The precise later course was not predictable in 2017, but its architecture became progressively foreseeable. The case is therefore not principally about a missed diagnosis or a missing symptom. It is about an unresolved relationship being converted into a durable management state, and a patient spending eight years living the consequences while the information needed to reopen that relationship repeatedly appeared without acquiring enough authority to displace the original arrangement.**

### The irreducible profile

> **One problem went in.**
> **Its components came back out.**
> **The relationship between them did not.**
> **The patient spent eight years living that missing relationship.**

And perhaps the sharpest single sentence is:

> **This is a case in which the medicine repeatedly got better at treating the parts while the patient got progressively worse from living inside the unresolved whole.**
